=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given N points (x_1, y_1), (x_2, y_2), \dots, (x_N, y_N) on a two-dimensional plane, and a non-negative integer D.
Find the number of integer pairs (x, y) such that \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N (|x-x_i|+|y-y_i|) \leq D.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N D
x_1 y_1
x_2 y_2
\vdots
x_N y_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq D \leq 10^6
- -10^6 \leq x_i, y_i \leq 10^6
- (x_i, y_i) \neq (x_j, y_j) for i \neq j.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 3
0 0
1 0

Sample Output 1

8

The following figure visualizes the input and the answer for Sample 1. The blue points represent the input. The blue and red points, eight in total, satisfy the condition in the statement.

Sample Input 2

2 0
0 0
2 0

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

6 100
9 -6
10 -1
2 10
-1 7
-7 5
-1 -4

Sample Output 3

419

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's formalize.

Given sorted xs list length N, prefix sums prefX where pref[0]=0, pref[k]=sum first k xs. Sum abs(x - xs[i]) = x*k - pref[k] + (pref[N]-pref[k]) - x*(N-k), where k = number of xs <= x (bisect_right(xs,x)). Let total=pref[N]. f(x)= x*k - pref[k] + (total - pref[k]) - x*(N-k) = x*(2k - N) + total - 2*pref[k]. This works for integer x.

Let's explore algorithms.

Option A: Generate sorted arrays of f values and g values for x/y satisfying <=D. Then count pairs with sum<=D using two-pointer. Need generate sorted arrays efficiently.

6. Find Lx,Rx:
   x0 = xs[N//2].
   lo = min(xs) - D  # maybe -2e6
   hi = max(xs) + D
   # But if lo > x0? no.

   l = lo; r = x0

       mid = (l+r)//2
       if f(mid) <= D:
           r = mid
       else:
           l = mid+1
   Lx = l
   # Rx: last x in [x0, hi] with f(x)<=D. f nondecreasing.
   l = x0; r = hi
   while l < r:

       if f(mid) <= D:
           l = mid
       else:
           r = mid-1
   Rx = l

Now, count = ry - ly + 1 if ly <= ry else 0. Since we maintain ly and ry, after expansions, if T < g_min, ly=y0+1, ry=y0-1, count=0. If T >= g_min, ly<=y0<=ry, count positive.

Let's design count_left and count_right separately, with a helper to initialize y state.

Initial y state function:
def init_y_state():
    ly = y0 + 1
    ry = y0 - 1
    l = y0
    qL = bisect_left(ys, y0)
    pL = bisect_right(ys, y0)
    g_l = l*pL - prefY[pL] + (totalY - prefY[pL]) - l*(N-pL)
    r = y0
    pR = pL
    g_r = g_l
    return ly, ry, l, qL, g_l, r, pR, g_r
But note pL and pR both same initial. qL maybe less. We don't need pL after g_l computed. Good.

But if y0 has duplicates, g_l = g_r = min. qL excludes duplicates, pR includes. Good.

Now, for left side loop:

   x = Lx

       # expand left

           g_l = g_l - (2*qL - N)
           l -= 1

           delta = 2*pR - N
           g_r += delta
           r += 1

       # move x to x+1
       deltaX = 2*pX - N
       f_cur += deltaX
       x += 1
       while pX < N and xs[pX] <= x:
           pX += 1

Need check order of x update: At current x, pX = # xs <= x. deltaX = f(x+1)-f(x). After f_cur += deltaX, x +=1, update pX to # <= new x. Good.

- Use local variables in loops: xs_l=xs, prefX_l=prefX, totalX_l=totalX, N_l=N, etc.

Let's write code skeleton:

import sys
from bisect import bisect_right, bisect_left

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,D = map(int,input().split())
    xs=[]; ys=[]
    for _ in range(N):
        x,y=map(int,input().split())
        xs.append(x); ys.append(y)
    xs.sort(); ys.sort()
    prefX=[0]*(N+1)
    for i,v in enumerate(xs):
        prefX[i+1]=prefX[i]+v
    prefY=[0]*(N+1)
    for i,v in enumerate(ys):
        prefY[i+1]=prefY[i]+v
    totalX=prefX[N]; totalY=prefY[N]

    def val_x(x):
        k=bisect_right(xs,x)
        return x*k - prefX[k] + (totalX - prefX[k]) - x*(N-k)
    def val_y(y):
        k=bisect_right(ys,y)
        return y*k - prefY[k] + (totalY - prefY[k]) - y*(N-k)

    x0 = xs[N//2]
    y0 = ys[N//2]
    if val_x(x0) + val_y(y0) > D:
        print(0); return

    # Find Lx
    lo = xs[0] - D
    hi = xs[-1] + D
    l = lo; r = x0
    while l < r:
        mid = (l+r)//2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid+1
    Lx = l
    l = x0; r = hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l+r+1)//2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            l = mid
        else:
            r = mid-1
    Rx = l

    ans = 0
    # maybe define a helper to initialize y state? But function returns tuple; called twice.
    # We'll inline for speed? Could define init_y_state returning tuple.
    def init_y_state():
        ly = y0 + 1
        ry = y0 - 1
        l = y0
        qL = bisect_left(ys, y0)
        pR = bisect_right(ys, y0)
        g_l = y0*pR - prefY[pR] + (totalY - prefY[pR]) - y0*(N-pR)
        g_r = g_l
        r = y0
        return ly, ry, l, qL, g_l, r, pR, g_r

    # Left side
    ly, ry, l, qL, g_l, r, pR, g_r = init_y_state()
    x = Lx
    pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
    f_cur = x*pX - prefX[pX] + (totalX - prefX[pX]) - x*(N-pX)
    # local bindings
    ys_local = ys; prefY_local = prefY; totalY_local = totalY; N_local=N
    # Actually inside loops, use local variables already.
    while x <= x0:
        T = D - f_cur
        # expand left
        while g_l <= T:
            ly = l
            g_l = g_l - (2*qL - N)
            l -= 1
            while qL > 0 and ys[qL-1] >= l:
                qL -= 1
        # expand right
        while g_r <= T:
            ry = r
            delta = 2*pR - N
            g_r += delta
            r += 1
            while pR < N and ys[pR] <= r:
                pR += 1
        if ly <= ry:
            ans += ry - ly + 1
        # advance x
        deltaX = 2*pX - N
        f_cur += deltaX
        x += 1
        while pX < N and xs[pX] <= x:
            pX += 1

    # Right side reverse
    if Rx > x0:
        ly, ry, l, qL, g_l, r, pR, g_r = init_y_state()
        x = Rx
        pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
        f_cur = x*pX - prefX[pX] + (totalX - prefX[pX]) - x*(N-pX)
        qX = bisect_right(xs, x-1)
        while x > x0:
            T = D - f_cur
            # expand left
            while g_l <= T:
                ly = l
                g_l = g_l - (2*qL - N)
                l -= 1
                while qL > 0 and ys[qL-1] >= l:
                    qL -= 1
            # expand right
            while g_r <= T:
                ry = r
                delta = 2*pR - N
                g_r += delta
                r += 1
                while pR < N and ys[pR] <= r:
                    pR += 1
            if ly <= ry:
                ans += ry - ly + 1
            # advance x downward
            deltaX = 2*qX - N
            f_cur -= deltaX
            x -= 1
            while qX > 0 and xs[qX-1] >= x:
                qX -= 1

    print(ans)

Need verify left side x update after last iteration: Suppose x=x0, process, then deltaX computed, x becomes x0+1, pX update. Loop exits. No issue. But pX update after x0 may include xs == x0+1. If x0+1 maybe >Rx? Doesn't matter. But if x0 = Rx, after last iteration pX update could access xs[pX] <= x0+1; pX maybe N, okay. Fine.

Need verify right side qX update after last iteration: Suppose x = x0+1, process, then deltaX computed, x becomes x0, qX update. Loop exits. No issue. But qX update after x becomes x0 uses while qX>0 and xs[qX-1] >= x0; if qX includes xs==x0, remove. Fine.

Now, potential bug in left expansion: We initialize g_l = g(y0) using pR (# <= y0). But qL = # < y0. For left expansion update, g_l = g_l - (2*qL - N). This formula uses qL = # ys < old l. Initially old l = y0, qL = # < y0. Correct. But what about if y0 has duplicates and qL excludes them; cnt_le_{y0-1} = # <= y0-1 = # < y0 = qL. Good. So g(y0-1) computed correctly. If y0-1 not integer? y0 integer. Good.

Right expansion update: g_r = g_r + (2*pR - N), pR = # <= old r. Initially r=y0, pR includes duplicates at y0. g(y0+1)-g(y0) = 2*cnt_le_y0 - N = 2*pR - N. Correct.

Now, need check if left expansion and right expansion share g_l/g_r but both initially same. If left while expands, it changes l,qL,g_l. Right while uses r,pR,g_r unaffected. Good.

But what if left while expands ly to less than y0, and then right while expands ry. The interval [ly,ry] includes y0. Count = ry-ly+1. But did we add all y between ly and y0? We added ly by moving left one step at a time from y0, so yes all intermediate y were added? Wait left while starts at l=y0 and adds y0, then l=y0-1, adds if <=T, etc. It adds every integer y from y0 down to ly, because l decrements by 1 each expansion. So yes. Right adds every integer from y0 up to ry. Thus interval [ly,ry] fully populated. Good.

Now, need consider if T increases and left while expands, then later right while expands. The interval may have ly < y0 and ry = y0-1? Actually right while will add y0 first, so ry becomes at least y0. If T>=g_min. If T<g_min, left didn't expand, right didn't. If T>=g_min, left may expand and right will expand. After both, interval valid. If left expands but right hasn't yet, during processing same x, we do left while then right while before counting, so both done. Good.

Now, what if T is so large that left while expands all the way, and then right while expands. The left while uses g_l computed for each new l. But note qL update while qL>0 and ys[qL-1] >= l. Since l decreases by 1 each expansion, qL may decrement multiple times if duplicate values at l. Let's test with ys=[0,0,0], N=3, y0=0, qL=0. Left expansion from 0 to -1: g_l = g(0) - (2*0-3)=g(0)+3. qL remains 0. l=-1. g(-1)=? ys all 0, g(-1)=3, g(0)=0? Wait g(0)=0, g(0)+3=3 correct. Next l=-2: qL=0, g_l = 3 - (0-3)=6 = g(-2)=6. Good.

Now, let's think about if `qL` update while `ys[qL-1] >= l` could remove elements that are not actually in interval? It maintains count of ys < l. Since l decreases, qL should decrease. But what if qL was not exactly #ys < l due to previous updates? Let's prove invariant:
At start of each left while iteration, l is current left candidate = ly - 1 (or y0 if empty), qL = # ys < l, g_l = g(l).
Initially l=y0, qL=bisect_left(ys,y0) = # < y0. Good.
After adding l:
- New ly = l.
- New l' = l - 1.
- g_l' = g(l') computed using old qL = # ys < l = # ys <= l-1 = cnt_le_{l'}. Formula g(l') = g(l) - (2*cnt_le_{l'} - N). Correct.
- qL' should be # ys < l' = # ys <= l-2. Old qL = # ys < l = # ys <= l-1. qL' = old qL - count(ys == l-1) = old qL - count(ys == l'). The while removes elements >= l' from the end of the prefix of elements < l. Since old qL counts all ys < l, the elements from index qL' to qL-1 are exactly those with values >= l' and < l, i.e., value l' (because integer). So while qL>0 and ys[qL-1] >= l' removes them. Good. If there are values < l' after removal, qL' correct. If l' is less than min_y, qL'=0. Good.
So invariant holds.

Right invariant:
At start of right while, r = ry + 1 (candidate), pR = # ys <= r, g_r = g(r).
Initially r=y0, pR=bisect_right(ys,y0). Good.
After adding r:
- New ry = r.
- New r' = r + 1.
- g_r' = g(r') = g(r) + (2*cnt_le_r - N) = g(r) + (2*pR - N). Correct.
- pR' = # ys <= r' = old pR + count(ys == r'). While pR<N and ys[pR] <= r' adds those. Good.

Now, f left invariant:
At start, x current, pX = # xs <= x, f_cur=f(x). After processing:
- delta = f(x+1)-f(x) = 2*pX - N. f_cur' = f(x+1).
- x' = x+1.
- pX' = # xs <= x+1 = old pX + count(ys == x+1). While xs[pX] <= x' adds. Good.

f right reverse invariant:
At start, x current, qX = # xs <= x-1, f_cur=f(x). After processing:
- delta = f(x)-f(x-1) = 2*qX - N. f_cur' = f(x-1).
- x' = x-1.
- qX' = # xs <= x'-1 = # xs <= x-2. Old qX = # xs <= x-1. qX' = old qX - count(xs == x-1) = old qX - count(xs == x'). While qX>0 and xs[qX-1] >= x' removes those. Good.

The problem is separable. We count x values with f(x)<=D. For each x, need count y with g(y)<=D-f(x). Since f is convex, x interval is contiguous. We split x into left of median (f nonincreasing) and right of median (f nondecreasing). Processing left in increasing x and right in decreasing x makes threshold T nondecreasing. For y, g is convex, so sublevel set is interval. As T increases, interval expands outward from median. We maintain interval endpoints and expand with O(1) per unit coordinate using sorted ys and prefix sums. Count is interval length. This counts each x once. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using median x0 = xs[N//2] for splitting but f might be nonincreasing on [Lx,x0] and nondecreasing on [x0,Rx]. For even N, upper median. Yes. For odd, median. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `g_l` initial formula using `pR` but if y0 has duplicates and pR > qL, g_l = g(y0). For left expansion, after adding y0, g_l becomes g(y0-1). But what if y0-1 is also a median (flat region) and has duplicates? qL = # < y0 includes all y0-1 duplicates. g_l formula after update: g(y0) - (2*qL - N). Let's test with ys=[0,1,1,2], N=4, y0=1 (index2? sorted [0,1,1,2], N//2=2, y0=1). qL=bisect_left(ys,1)=1 (#<1: 0), pR=bisect_right(ys,1)=3. g(1)=|1-0|+0+0+|1-2|=2. g(0)=0+1+1+2=4? If lower median=1, upper=1, no flat interval. If ys=[0,1,2,3], N=4, y0=2 (index2), qL=bisect_left(ys,2)=2, pR=3? Wait sorted [0,1,2,3], bisect_right(2)=3. g(2)=|2|+|1|+0+1=4. g(1)=1+0+1+2=4. Formula g(2)-(2*qL-N)=4-(4-4)=4. Good. Flat. qL=2 (#<2:0,1), cnt_le_{1}=2. Good.

Good. g_l=4 = g(1). Next add l=1: g_l = 4 - (2*1-4)=6 = g(0)=0+1+2+3=6. l=0, qL update while qL>0 and ys[0]=0>=0 -> qL=0. Good.

Let's maybe consider if we can reduce duplication by defining a local function `expand_y(T)` that uses `nonlocal` variables. But in Python, nonlocal inside main nested function works. However, we need two separate states for left and right. Could define a class? Overkill. Inline.

Let's draft final code:

```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right, bisect_left

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, D = map(int, input().split())
    xs = [0]*N
    ys = [0]*N
    for i in range(N):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        xs[i] = x
        ys[i] = y
    xs.sort()
    ys.sort()
    prefX = [0]*(N+1)
    for i, v in enumerate(xs):
        prefX[i+1] = prefX[i] + v
    prefY = [0]*(N+1)
    for i, v in enumerate(ys):
        prefY[i+1] = prefY[i] + v
    totalX = prefX[N]
    totalY = prefY[N]

    def val_x(x):
        k = bisect_right(xs, x)
        return x*k - prefX[k] + (totalX - prefX[k]) - x*(N-k)

    def val_y(y):
        k = bisect_right(ys, y)
        return y*k - prefY[k] + (totalY - prefY[k]) - y*(N-k)

    x0 = xs[N//2]
    y0 = ys[N//2]
    if val_x(x0) + val_y(y0) > D:
        print(0)
        return

    lo = xs[0] - D
    hi = xs[-1] + D

    l = lo
    r = x0
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1
    Lx = l

    l = x0
    r = hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r + 1) // 2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            l = mid
        else:
            r = mid - 1
    Rx = l

    ans = 0

    # helper to initialize y-state
    def init_y():
        ly = y0 + 1
        ry = y0 - 1
        lc = y0
        qL = bisect_left(ys, y0)
        pR = bisect_right(ys, y0)
        g_l = y0 * pR - prefY[pR] + (totalY - prefY[pR]) - y0 * (N - pR)
        g_r = g_l
        rc = y0
        return ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r

    # Left side: x increasing from Lx to x0
    ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r = init_y()
    x = Lx
    pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
    f_cur = x * pX - prefX[pX] + (totalX - prefX[pX]) - x * (N - pX)

    while x <= x0:
        T = D - f_cur

        while g_l <= T:
            ly = lc
            g_l = g_l - (2 * qL - N)
            lc -= 1
            while qL > 0 and ys[qL - 1] >= lc:
                qL -= 1

        while g_r <= T:
            ry = rc
            delta = 2 * pR - N
            g_r += delta
            rc += 1
            while pR < N and ys[pR] <= rc:
                pR += 1

        if ly <= ry:
            ans += ry - ly + 1

        deltaX = 2 * pX - N
        f_cur += deltaX
        x += 1
        while pX < N and xs[pX] <= x:
            pX += 1

    # Right side: x decreasing from Rx to x0+1
    if Rx > x0:
        ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r = init_y()
        x = Rx
        pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
        f_cur = x * pX - prefX[pX] + (totalX - prefX[pX]) - x * (N - pX)
        qX = bisect_right(xs, x - 1)

        while x > x0:
            T = D - f_cur

            while g_l <= T:
                ly = lc
                g_l = g_l - (2 * qL - N)
                lc -= 1
                while qL > 0 and ys[qL - 1] >= lc:
                    qL -= 1

            while g_r <= T:
                ry = rc
                delta = 2 * pR - N
                g_r += delta
                rc += 1
                while pR < N and ys[pR] <= rc:
                    pR += 1

            if ly <= ry:
                ans += ry - ly + 1

            deltaX = 2 * qX - N
            f_cur -= deltaX
            x -= 1
            while qX > 0 and xs[qX - 1] >= x:
                qX -= 1

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Need check variable names: `lc` for left candidate l, `rc` for right candidate r. Good.

Potential issue: In `init_y`, `g_l` computed using `pR`; but `qL` is bisect_left. If y0 has duplicates, pR > qL. For left expansion, `g_l` initial = g(y0). Good. But what about if y0 is not the first occurrence and qL excludes duplicates, but `g_l` formula uses pR includes duplicates. Good.

Now, let's think about if in `init_y`, `g_r = g_l`. Good.

Now, let's consider if in left side loop, after processing x, we update f_cur and x even if x==x0. This means after loop, x=x0+1. But if x0+1 > Rx, no issue. However, if x0+1 is within right side, we don't process it in left; right side will process from Rx down to x0+1. Good. But the left loop's final update computed f_cur at x0+1 and pX updated; these variables are not used after loop because we reinitialize for right side. So no issue.

Now, let's think about if in right side loop, after processing x, we update f_cur and x even if x==x0+1. After loop, x=x0. Variables not used. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with `qX = bisect_right(xs, x - 1)` when x=Rx and Rx maybe very large; if x-1 >= max_x, qX=N. Good.

Now, let's think about if in right side loop, `deltaX = 2 * qX - N` and `f_cur -= deltaX`. If qX=N, deltaX=N, f decreases by N when moving left. Correct for x > max_x. Good.

Now, let's consider if in left side loop, `deltaX = 2 * pX - N` and `f_cur += deltaX`. If pX=0, deltaX=-N, f decreases by N when moving right. Correct for x < min_x. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `while pX < N and xs[pX] <= x` after x increment. If x is large and pX=N, condition false. If pX points to xs value equal to new x, adds. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with `while qX > 0 and xs[qX - 1] >= x` after x decrement. If qX=0, false. If qX points to element equal to new x, removes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `bisect_right` in `val_x` for x=-2e6 and xs=[-1e6], k=0, f= -2e6*0 -0 + (-1e6)
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right, bisect_left

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, D = map(int, input().split())

    xs = [0] * N
    ys = [0] * N
    for i in range(N):
        x, y = map(int, input().split())
        xs[i] = x
        ys[i] = y

    xs.sort()
    ys.sort()

    pref_x = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, v in enumerate(xs):
        pref_x[i + 1] = pref_x[i] + v

    pref_y = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, v in enumerate(ys):
        pref_y[i + 1] = pref_y[i] + v

    total_x = pref_x[N]
    total_y = pref_y[N]

    def val_x(x):
        k = bisect_right(xs, x)
        return x * k - pref_x[k] + (total_x - pref_x[k]) - x * (N - k)

    def val_y(y):
        k = bisect_right(ys, y)
        return y * k - pref_y[k] + (total_y - pref_y[k]) - y * (N - k)

    x0 = xs[N // 2]
    y0 = ys[N // 2]

    # Minimum possible value of f(x) + g(y)
    if val_x(x0) + val_y(y0) > D:
        print(0)
        return

    # Find the interval of x with f(x) <= D
    lo = xs[0] - D
    hi = xs[-1] + D

    l, r = lo, x0
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r) // 2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            r = mid
        else:
            l = mid + 1
    Lx = l

    l, r = x0, hi
    while l < r:
        mid = (l + r + 1) // 2
        if val_x(mid) <= D:
            l = mid
        else:
            r = mid - 1
    Rx = l

    ans = 0

    def init_y_state():
        # Empty interval around the median y0.
        ly = y0 + 1
        ry = y0 - 1

        # Left expansion state
        lc = y0
        qL = bisect_left(ys, y0)          # number of ys < lc
        pR = bisect_right(ys, y0)         # number of ys <= y0
        g_l = y0 * pR - pref_y[pR] + (total_y - pref_y[pR]) - y0 * (N - pR)

        # Right expansion state
        rc = y0
        g_r = g_l

        return ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r

    # Left side: x increases from Lx to x0, so T = D - f(x) is nondecreasing.
    ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r = init_y_state()

    x = Lx
    pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
    f_cur = x * pX - pref_x[pX] + (total_x - pref_x[pX]) - x * (N - pX)

    while x <= x0:
        T = D - f_cur

        # Expand y-interval to the left while possible.
        while g_l <= T:
            ly = lc
            g_l = g_l - (2 * qL - N)
            lc -= 1
            while qL > 0 and ys[qL - 1] >= lc:
                qL -= 1

        # Expand y-interval to the right while possible.
        while g_r <= T:
            ry = rc
            delta = 2 * pR - N
            g_r += delta
            rc += 1
            while pR < N and ys[pR] <= rc:
                pR += 1

        if ly <= ry:
            ans += ry - ly + 1

        # Move x to x + 1.
        delta_x = 2 * pX - N
        f_cur += delta_x
        x += 1
        while pX < N and xs[pX] <= x:
            pX += 1

    # Right side: process x from Rx down to x0+1, so T is nondecreasing.
    if Rx > x0:
        ly, ry, lc, qL, g_l, rc, pR, g_r = init_y_state()

        x = Rx
        pX = bisect_right(xs, x)
        f_cur = x * pX - pref_x[pX] + (total_x - pref_x[pX]) - x * (N - pX)
        qX = bisect_right(xs, x - 1)      # number of xs <= x-1

        while x > x0:
            T = D - f_cur

            while g_l <= T:
                ly = lc
                g_l = g_l - (2 * qL - N)
                lc -= 1
                while qL > 0 and ys[qL - 1] >= lc:
                    qL -= 1

            while g_r <= T:
                ry = rc
                delta = 2 * pR - N
                g_r += delta
                rc += 1
                while pR < N and ys[pR] <= rc:
                    pR += 1

            if ly <= ry:
                ans += ry - ly + 1

            # Move x to x - 1.
            delta_x = 2 * qX - N
            f_cur -= delta_x
            x -= 1
            while qX > 0 and xs[qX - 1] >= x:
                qX -= 1

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
